Recalling that
\Theta_{D_{k}}
are isometries, we get

(26)

\displaystyle|\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(y))-\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(z))|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{y_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}}{|\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{y_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}|}-\frac{\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{z_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}}{|\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{z_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}|}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq(2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{k})\|)^{s(x)}\Big|\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{y_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}-\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{z_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}\Big|.

Substituting this back in (
25
) yields
\Big(
recall Hadamard’s inequality, which asserts that if a volume form
\widetilde{\omega}
is the wedge product
\widetilde{\omega}=u_{1}\wedge...\wedge u_{l}
, then
|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq\prod_{i=1}^{l}|u_{i}|_{2}\Big)
,

\displaystyle A\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big|\Theta_{D_{k+1}}d_{f^{k}(y)}f\nu_{f^{k}(y)}\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(y))-\Theta_{D_{k+1}}d_{f^{k}(z)}f\nu_{f^{k}(z)}\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(y))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Theta_{D_{k+1}}d_{f^{k}(z)}f\nu_{f^{k}(z)}\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(y))-\Theta_{D_{k+1}}d_{f^{k}(z)}f\nu_{f^{k}(z)}\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(z))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d_{f^{k}(z)}f\|^{s(x)}\cdot|\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(y))-\Theta_{D_{k}}\omega_{s}(f^{k}(z))|+\|\Theta_{D_{k+1}}d_{f^{k}(y)}f\nu_{f^{k}(y)}-\Theta_{D_{k+1}}d_{f^{k}(z)}f\nu_{f^{k}(z)}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(M_{f}^{d}\cdot(2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{k})\|)^{s(x)}\cdot\Big|\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{y_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}-\Theta_{D_{k}}d_{z_{k}}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k})\omega_{1}\Big|+H_{0}d(f^{k}(y),f^{k}(z))^{\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(M_{f}^{d}\cdot(2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{k})\|)^{s(x)}\cdot[\text{H\"{o}l}_{\frac{\beta}{2}}(d_{\cdot}(\psi_{x_{k}}\circ G_{k}))|y_{k}-z_{k}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}]^{s(x)}+H_{0}d(f^{k}(y),f^{k}(z))^{\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(M_{f}^{d}\cdot(2\cdot\frac{1}{2})^{s(x)}(2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{k})\||y_{k}-z_{k}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}})^{s(x)}+H_{0}d(f^{k}(y),f^{k}(z))^{\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(M_{f}^{d}(2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}}|y_{k}-z_{k}|^{\frac{\beta}{4}})^{s(x)}+H_{0}d(f^{k}(y),f^{k}(z))^{\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(M_{f}^{d}\cdot(2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}}(2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot(I^{-k}(p_{0}^{s}))^{2}\cdot\frac{d(y,z)}{(p_{0}^{s})^{2}})^{\frac{\beta}{4}})+H_{0}(C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\cdot 2(I^{-k}(p_{0}^{s}))^{1-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\cdot\frac{d(y,z)}{p_{0}^{s}})^{\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{f}^{d}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(M_{f}^{d}\cdot(2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}}((I^{-k}(p_{0}^{s}))^{2}\cdot\frac{d(y,z)}{(p_{0}^{s})^{3}})^{\frac{\beta}{4}})+H_{0}(C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\cdot 2(I^{-k}(p_{0}^{s}))^{1-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\cdot\frac{d(y,z)}{p_{0}^{s}})^{\beta}\Big)

(27)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3M_{f}^{2d}H_{0}\cdot C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{4},\frac{1}{\gamma}\}}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}(I^{-k}(p_{0}^{s}))^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\cdot(\frac{d(y,z)}{(p_{0}^{s})^{3}})^{\frac{\beta}{4}}\leq\epsilon^{2}\cdot(\frac{d(y,z)}{(p_{0}^{s})^{3}})^{\frac{\beta}{4}},

where Höl
{}_{\frac{\beta}{2}}(d_{\cdot}G_{k})\leq\frac{1}{2}
, and Lip(
d_{\cdot}\psi_{x_{k}})\leq 2
since
\|C_{0}(x_{k})\|\leq 1
. The seventh inequality in (
4.1.1
) uses the fact that
M_{f}>1
, and the penultimate inequality uses the fact that
|y_{k}-z_{k}|<1
to replace
s(x)
with
1
, and uses (
23
).

By the choice of
Q_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
(Definition
2.12
) and the inequality
p^{s}_{k}\leq Q_{\epsilon}(x_{k})
, and since
\gamma>\frac{2}{\beta}
, we have
\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{k})\|\cdot(p_{k}^{s})^{\frac{\beta}{4}}<C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}}
for all
k\geq 0
. In addition, Lemma
2.6
tells us that
\sum_{k\geq 0}(I^{-k}(p_{0}^{s}))^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq\sum_{k\geq 0}(I^{-k}(1))^{\frac{\beta}{2}}<\infty
. So for small enough
\epsilon
, (
4.1.1
) holds.
This estimate is uniform in
n
, and Part (3) is proven.

∎

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
V^{s}
be an admissible
s
-manifold in
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
which stays in windows. Then
\forall y\in V^{s}
,

S^{2}(y)\leq 2\cdot 4\frac{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|}{p^{s}}\sum_{m\geq 0}(I^{-m}(1))^{2}<\infty.

A similar bound holds for
U^{2}(\cdot)
on
u
-manifolds which stay in windows.

Notice
: In a setup where we are given two double charts
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}\rightarrow\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
, if
V^{s}
is an
s
-admissible manifold in
\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
which stays in windows, then also
\mathcal{F}(V^{s})
is an
s
-admissible manifold in
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
which stays in windows.
For the next lemma, recall Definition
2.9
for
S(\cdot,\cdot)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}\rightarrow\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
. Let
V^{s}
be an
s
-admissible manifold in
\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
which stays in windows. Let
\mathcal{F}(V^{s})
be the Graph Transform of
V^{s}
w.r.t to the edge
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}\rightarrow\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
. Let
q\in f[\mathcal{F}(V^{s})]\subset V^{s}
.
Then

\exists\pi_{x}:T_{f^{-1}(q)}\mathcal{F}(V^{s})\rightarrow H^{s}(x),\pi_{y}:T_{q}V^{s}\rightarrow H^{s}(y)
invertible linear transformations s.t
\forall\rho\geq e^{\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x)}
: if
\frac{S(q,\lambda)}{S(y,\pi_{y}\lambda)}\in[\rho^{-1},\rho]
then

\frac{S(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}\in[e^{\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}\rho^{-1},e^{-\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}\rho],

where
\lambda=d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi
and
\xi\in T_{f^{-1}(q)}\mathcal{F}(V^{s})
is arbitrary.
A similar statement holds for the unstable case. We will also see that
\frac{|\pi_{x}\xi|}{|\xi|}=e^{\pm Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}
for all
\xi\in T_{f^{-1}(q)}\mathcal{F}(V^{s})
(similarly for
\pi_{y}
).

Proof.

Recall the disks
\mathcal{D}=\{D\}
and the isometries
\Theta_{D}:TD\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d},\nu_{a}=\Theta_{D}^{-1}|_{T_{a}M},a\in D\subset M
from Definition
2.16
. If
\epsilon
is smaller than the Lebesgue number of
\mathcal{D}
, then
\exists D
which contains
x,f^{-1}(q),f^{-1}(y)
. Let
\Theta=\Theta_{D}
, and set
C_{1}:=\Theta\circ C_{0}(x),C_{2}:=\Theta\circ C_{0}(f^{-1}(y))
.
Let
F,G
denote the representing functions of
V^{s},\mathcal{F}(V^{s})
, respectively.

Let
\xi\in T_{f^{-1}(q)}\mathcal{F}(V^{s})
. Write
t=\psi_{x}^{-1}(f^{-1}(q))=(t_{s},G(t_{s}))
, and

\xi=d_{t}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{t_{s}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)
for a vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{s(x)}
. We define

\pi_{x}\xi:=d_{(0,0)}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
0\\
\end{array}\Big)
(
\pi_{y}
is defined analogously). Denote by
E_{0}
the
|\cdot|_{\infty}
-Lipschitz constant of
(x,\underline{u},\underline{v})\mapsto[\Theta_{D}\circ d_{\underline{v}}\exp][\nu_{x}\underline{u}]
.

We begin with bounding the distortion of
\pi_{x}
(assume w.l.o.g
6
6

6

Otherwise cancel its size from both the denominator and numerator of the LHS of eq. (
41
).

|\xi|=1
):

\displaystyle|\Theta\xi-\Theta\pi_{x}\xi|=

\displaystyle|\Theta d_{(t_{s},G(t_{s}))}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{t_{s}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)-\Theta d_{(0,0)}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
0\\
\end{array}\Big)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\Theta\Big(d_{C_{0}(x)(t_{s},G(t_{s}))}\exp_{x}\Big)\nu_{x}\circ C_{1}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{t_{s}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)-\Theta\Big(d_{C_{0}(x)(0,0)}\exp_{x}\Big)\nu_{x}\circ C_{1}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
0\\
\end{array}\Big)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{0}\Big(\Big|C_{1}\Big(\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{t_{s}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)-\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
0\\
\end{array}\Big)\Big)\Big|+\Big|C_{0}(x)\Big((t_{s},G(t_{s}))-(0,0)\Big)\Big|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{0}\Big(|v|\cdot\|d_{t_{s}}G\|+|\exp_{x}^{-1}f^{-1}(q)|\Big)=E_{0}\Big(|v|\cdot\|d_{t_{s}}G\|+d(f^{-1}(q),x)\Big)(\because C_{1}\text{ is a contraction})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3E_{0}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\beta/2}\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\leq 3E_{0}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\text{ }(\because|v|\leq 2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|,\|d_{t_{s}}G\|\leq Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\beta/2}).

Therefore
\frac{|\pi_{x}\xi|}{|\xi|}=e^{\pm Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}
(similarly,
\frac{|\pi_{y}\xi^{\prime}|}{|\xi^{\prime}|}=e^{\pm Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}},\xi^{\prime}\in T_{q}V^{s}\setminus\{0\}
). We continue to decompose:

(40)

\frac{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}{S(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}=\frac{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}{S(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}\cdot\frac{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)},

where
\eta:=d_{y}(f^{-1})\pi_{y}d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi\in H^{s}(f^{-1}(y))
(
\|\pi_{y}\|\leq e^{Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}(y)}
, hence
|\eta|\leq 2M_{f}^{2}
).
We study the second factor. Denote

u_{1}:=C_{0}^{-1}(x)\xi,u_{2}:=C_{0}^{-1}(f^{-1}(y))\eta.

By the definitions of
S(\cdot,\cdot)
and
C_{0}(\cdot)
(Definition
2.9
, Theorem
2.2
), the inverse of the second factor satisfies:

(41)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}-1\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{|C_{0}^{-1}(f^{-1}(y))\eta|}{|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\pi_{x}\xi|}-1\Big|=\Big|\frac{|C_{2}^{-1}\Theta\eta|}{|C_{1}^{-1}\Theta\pi_{x}\xi|}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{|C_{2}^{-1}\Theta\eta-C_{1}^{-1}\Theta\eta|+|C_{1}^{-1}\Theta\eta-C_{1}^{-1}\Theta\pi_{x}\xi|+|C_{1}^{-1}\Theta\pi_{x}\xi|}{|C_{1}^{-1}\Theta\pi_{x}\xi|}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\pi_{x}\xi|}\Big(\|C_{2}^{-1}-C_{1}^{-1}\|\cdot|\eta|+\|C_{1}^{-1}\|\cdot|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\eta|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Big(2M_{f}^{2}Q_{\epsilon}^{4}(x)Q_{\epsilon}^{4}(f^{-1}(y))+\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\eta|\Big)

\displaystyle(\because

\displaystyle\|C_{0}(x)\|\leq 1\text{, Lemma \ref{overlap}},|\eta|\leq 2M_{f}^{2},|\pi_{x}\xi|\geq e^{-2Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}(x)}\geq\frac{1}{2}).

By the definition of
\omega_{0}
(Lemma
2.15
),
\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(f^{-1}(y))}{Q_{\epsilon}(y)}\leq\omega_{0}
. Substituting this gives:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}-1\Big|\leq

\displaystyle 4M_{f}^{2}\omega_{0}Q_{\epsilon}^{4}(x)Q_{\epsilon}^{4}(y)+2\cdot\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\eta|

(42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}Q_{\epsilon}^{3}(x)Q_{\epsilon}^{3}(y)+2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\eta|,

for small enough
\epsilon
. Next we bound
|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\eta|
:

Step 1
:
|\Theta\xi-\Theta\pi_{x}\xi|\leq 3E_{0}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\leq\frac{1}{2}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}
by (
4.1.1
).

Step 2
: Estimation of
|\Theta\xi-\Theta\eta|
. Let
\lambda:=d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi
and
\hat{\lambda}=\frac{\lambda}{|\lambda|}

7
7

7

\hat{\lambda}
is well defined since
f
is a diffeomorphism and
|\xi|\neq 0
.
.
Then

\displaystyle|\Theta\eta-\Theta\xi|\equiv

\displaystyle|\Theta d_{y}(f^{-1})\pi_{y}d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi-\Theta\xi|=|\Theta d_{y}(f^{-1})\pi_{y}d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi-\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\Theta d_{y}(f^{-1})\pi_{y}\lambda-\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})\lambda|=|\Theta d_{y}(f^{-1})\nu_{y}\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\lambda-\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})\nu_{q}\Theta^{\prime}\lambda|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Theta d_{y}(f^{-1})\nu_{y}\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\lambda-\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})\nu_{q}\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\lambda|+|\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})\nu_{q}\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\lambda-\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})\nu_{q}\Theta^{\prime}\lambda|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\Theta d_{y}(f^{-1})\nu_{y}-\Theta d_{q}(f^{-1})\nu_{q}\|\cdot|\pi_{y}\lambda|+M_{f}|\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\lambda-\Theta^{\prime}\lambda|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle H_{0}\cdot d(q,y)^{\beta}|\pi_{y}\lambda|+M_{f}|\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\hat{\lambda}-\Theta^{\prime}\hat{\lambda}|\cdot|\lambda|,

where
\Theta^{\prime}
is the linear isometry for the neighborhood containing
y
and
q
, as in Definition
2.16
, and
H_{0}
is the bound for Höl
(d_{\cdot}f^{-1})
as in the proof of Proposition
4.4
.

|\lambda|\leq M_{f}
. As in (
4.1.1
),
|\Theta^{\prime}\pi_{y}\hat{\lambda}-\Theta^{\prime}\hat{\lambda}|\leq 3E_{0}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}
, and hence also
|\pi_{y}\lambda|=|\pi_{y}\hat{\lambda}|\cdot|\lambda|\leq(1+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})\cdot M_{f}<2M_{f}
. Therefore,

|\Theta\eta-\Theta\xi|\leq M_{f}\cdot(2H_{0}\cdot Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\beta}+M_{f}\cdot 3E_{0}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})<2M_{f}^{2}(H_{0}+3E_{0})C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}.

Since
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}\rightarrow\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
,
\psi_{f(x)}^{p^{s}\wedge p^{u}}
and
\psi_{y}^{q^{s}\wedge q^{u}}

I
-overlap. Hence Lemma
2.18
gives us the bound

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(y)}{Q_{\epsilon}(x)}=

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(f(x))}{Q_{\epsilon}(x)}\cdot\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(y)}{Q_{\epsilon}(f(x))}\leq\omega_{0}\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(y)}{Q_{\epsilon}(f(x))}\leq\omega_{0}\Big(\frac{\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(f(x))\|}\Big)^{-2\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\omega_{0}(e^{Q_{\epsilon}(f(x))Q_{\epsilon}(y)})^{2\gamma}\leq\omega_{0}e^{\epsilon}\text{ (for small enough }\epsilon\text{)}.

Thus

|\Theta\eta-\Theta\xi|\leq 2M_{f}^{2}(H_{0}+3E_{0})(e^{\epsilon}\omega_{0})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\text{ for all }\epsilon\text{ enough}.

Step 3
: Adding the estimates in steps 1 and 2, we obtain

\displaystyle|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\eta|\leq

\displaystyle|\Theta\pi_{x}\xi-\Theta\xi|+|\Theta\xi-\Theta\eta|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}(\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}+3E_{0}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}})\leq C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}.

Substituting this in (
4.1.1
) gives (for small enough
\epsilon
):

(43)

\Big|\frac{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{1}{4}Q^{3}_{\epsilon}(x)Q^{3}_{\epsilon}(y)+2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}},

and hence,

\frac{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}=e^{\pm C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}.

This concludes the study of the second factor of the RHS of (
40
).

It remains to study the first factor of the RHS of (
40
). We begin with the identity

\displaystyle S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|d_{f^{-1}(y)}f^{m}\eta|^{2}=2\Big(|\eta|^{2}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}|d_{y}f^{m-1}d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta|^{2}\Big)=2|\eta|^{2}+S^{2}(y,d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta).

Similarly, and by the assumption of the lemma :

\displaystyle S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)=

\displaystyle 2|\xi|^{2}+S^{2}(q,d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi)\leq 2|\xi|^{2}+\rho^{2}S^{2}(y,\pi_{y}d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi).

By the choice of
\eta
,
d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta=\pi_{y}d_{f^{-1}(q)}f\xi
, so

S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)\leq 2|\xi|^{2}+\rho^{2}S^{2}(y,d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta)

and so,

\displaystyle\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2|\xi|^{2}+\rho^{2}S^{2}(y,d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta)}{2|\eta|^{2}+S^{2}(y,d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta)}=\rho^{2}-\frac{2(\rho^{2}-1)|\eta|^{2}+2(|\eta|^{2}-|\xi|^{2})}{2|\eta|^{2}+S^{2}(y,d_{f^{-1}(y)}f\eta)}

(44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{2}-\frac{2(\rho^{2}-1)|\eta|^{2}+2(|\eta|^{2}-|\xi|^{2})}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}.

By the bound achieved in step 2, and since
|\xi|=1
,
|\eta|=e^{\pm C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}
(for small enough
\epsilon
). Hence
\Big||\xi|^{2}-|\eta|^{2}\Big|\leq 2(e^{C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}-1)\leq 4C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}
. We get that for
\epsilon
small enough, since
Q_{\epsilon}(x)\ll\epsilon
,

\displaystyle\rho^{2}-1

\displaystyle\geq e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}-1\geq 2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle(\rho^{2}-1)|\eta|^{2}+(|\xi|^{2}-|\eta|^{2})\geq(\rho^{2}-1)|\eta|^{2}-4C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}=(\rho^{2}-1)[|\eta|^{2}-\frac{4C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}{\rho^{2}-1}]

\displaystyle\geq(\rho^{2}-1)[e^{-2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}-\frac{4C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{4\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}]\geq(\rho^{2}-1)e^{-C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}.

Plugging this in (
44
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq\rho^{2}-\frac{2(\rho^{2}-1)e^{-C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq\rho^{2}-\frac{2(\rho^{2}-1)e^{-2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}}{S^{2}(x,\pi_{x}\xi)},

where the last inequality is because, as we saw above,
\frac{S(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}{S(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}=e^{\pm C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

. So

\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq\rho^{2}-\frac{2(\rho^{2}-1)e^{-2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|^{2}\cdot|\pi_{x}\xi|^{2}}.

Substituting
|\xi|=1
,
\frac{|\pi_{x}\xi|}{|\xi|}=e^{\pm Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}
gives:

\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq\rho^{2}-\frac{2(\rho^{2}-1)e^{-2C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|^{2}\cdot e^{2Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}}\leq\rho^{2}\Big(1-\frac{2(1-\frac{1}{\rho^{2}})e^{-3C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|^{2}}\Big)

For
\rho\geq e^{\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x)}
,
1-\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\geq 1-e^{-2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x)}\geq\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x)
, so:

\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq\rho^{2}\Big(1-\frac{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x)e^{-3C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}}}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|^{2}}\Big).

By definition,
Q_{\epsilon}(x)\leq\frac{C_{\beta,\epsilon}}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|^{2\gamma}}
. Hence,

\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq\rho^{2}(1-3\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}+\frac{1}{\gamma}})\leq\rho^{2}e^{-3\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}.

Similarly,
\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\geq\rho^{-2}e^{3\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}
.

In summary,
\frac{S^{2}(x,\pi_{x}\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}
is the product of two factors (see (
40
)). The second factor is bounded by
e^{\pm C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}
,
and the first factor takes values in
[\rho^{-2}e^{3\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}},\rho^{2}e^{-3\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}]
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\leq

\displaystyle\rho^{2}e^{-3\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}+C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho^{2}e^{-2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}(x)\left(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}}{2\Gamma}Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}-\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{3}{4\gamma}}(x)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{2}e^{-2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}(x)\left(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{5\gamma}}}{2\Gamma}Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x)\right)}\leq\rho^{2}\Big(e^{-\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}\Big)^{2}.

and
\frac{S^{2}(f^{-1}(q),\xi)}{S^{2}(f^{-1}(y),\eta)}\geq\rho^{-2}\Big(e^{\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}}\Big)^{2}
for small enough
\epsilon
. This concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma 4.7
.

The following holds for any
\epsilon
small enough. For any two chains
(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p^{s}_{i},p^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}},(\psi_{y_{i}}^{q^{s}_{i},q^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
with each having some symbol repeating infinitely often in the future, if
\pi[(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p^{s}_{i},p^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}]=\pi[(\psi_{y_{i}}^{q^{s}_{i},q^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}]=p
then
\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and for any
\xi\in T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}
,

\frac{S(x_{k},\pi_{x,k}\xi)}{S(y_{k},\pi_{y,k}\xi)}=e^{\pm 2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})},

where
\pi_{x,k},\pi_{y,k}
denote respectively the maps
\pi_{x_{k}}:T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}\rightarrow H^{s}(x_{k}),\pi_{y_{k}}:T_{f^{k}(p)}U^{s}_{k}\rightarrow H^{s}(y_{k})
as in Lemma
4.6
,
and
V^{s}_{k}=V^{s}((\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\geq k}),U^{s}_{k}=V^{s}((\psi_{y_{i}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}})_{i\geq k})
.
A similar statement holds for the unstable manifold.

Remark
: Notice that
T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}=T_{f^{k}(p)}U^{s}_{k}
, which makes the choice and use of
\xi
well defined.
This can be seen by the facts that the orbit
(f^{k}(p))_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
stays on the intersection of stable and unstable manifolds, and Proposition
4.4
(2) gives lower bounds for the contraction/expansion by the differential on the respective manifolds’ tangent spaces. Thus, the stable space of
f^{k}(p)
(which exists by Proposition
4.4
(2))
coincides with
T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}
, and
T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}=\text{stable space of }f^{k}(p)=T_{f^{k}(p)}U^{s}_{k}
.

Proof.

Denote
\vec{v}=(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p^{s}_{i},p^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
\vec{u}=(\psi_{y_{i}}^{q^{s}_{i},q^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
.
V^{s}_{k}
and
U^{s}_{k}
are
s
-admissible manifolds which stay in windows. We will prove that:

(45)

\forall\text{ }\xi\in T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}=T_{f^{k}(p)}U^{s}_{k}:\text{ }\frac{S(f^{k}(p),\xi)}{S(x_{k},\pi_{x,k}\xi)},\frac{S(f^{k}(p),\xi)}{S(y_{k},\pi_{y,k}\xi)}=e^{\pm\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}.

This is sufficient, since

\frac{S(x_{k},\pi_{x,k}\xi)}{S(y_{k},\pi_{y,k}\xi)}=\frac{S(x_{k},\pi_{x,k}\xi)}{S(f^{k}(p),\xi)}\cdot\frac{S(f^{k}(p),\xi)}{S(y_{k},\pi_{y,k}\xi)}.

We show
\frac{S(f^{k}(p),\xi)}{S(x_{k},\pi_{x,k}\xi)}=e^{\pm\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}
; the case of
\frac{S(f^{k}(p),\xi)}{S(y_{k},\pi_{y,k}\xi)}
is identical. By assumption, there exists a double chart
v
and a sequence
n_{k}\uparrow\infty
s.t
v_{n_{k}}=v
for all
k
. Write
v=\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
.

For all
k\geq 0
,
f^{n_{k}}(p)\in V^{s}(\sigma^{n_{k}}\underline{v})
, and by Corollary
4.5
,
\exists C_{v}>0
s.t
S(f^{n_{k}}(p))\leq C_{v}
. Set

k_{v}:=\lceil\frac{\log C_{v}}{\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}(x)}\rceil.

Let
\xi\in T_{f^{n_{k_{v}}}(p)}V^{s}(\sigma^{n_{k_{v}}}\underline{v})(1)
. Pulling back
n_{k_{v}}
times, Lemma
4.6
tells us that for every
j\in\{0,\ldots,n_{k_{v}}\}
either
\frac{S(f^{j}(p),d_{f^{n_{k_{v}}}}f^{-(n_{k_{v}}-j)}\xi)}{S(x_{j},\pi_{x,j}d_{f^{n_{k_{v}}}}f^{-(n_{k_{v}}-j)}\xi)}=e^{\pm\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x_{j})}
, or the ratio improves by
e^{-\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}+\frac{3}{4\gamma}}(x)}
. However, the ratio starts from a quantity bounded by
C_{v}
. It follow that there must be
j\in\{0,\ldots,n_{k_{v}}\}
s.t

\frac{S(f^{j}(p),d_{f^{n_{k_{v}}}}f^{-(n_{k_{v}}-j)}\xi)}{S(x_{j},\pi_{x,j}d_{f^{n_{k_{v}}}}f^{-(n_{k_{v}}-j)}\xi)}=e^{\pm\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x_{j})}.

With every further iteration, the ratio can only improve, or remain bounded by
e^{\pm C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(x_{j})}
(recall (
43
)). This holds for every
\xi\in T_{f^{n_{k_{v}}}(p)}V^{s}(\sigma^{n_{k_{v}}}\underline{v})(1)
, and so
\forall\xi^{\prime}\in T_{p}V^{s}(\underline{v})(1)

\frac{S(p,\xi^{\prime})}{S(x_{0},\pi_{x,0}\xi^{\prime})}=e^{\pm\max\{C_{\beta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(x_{0}),\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}}(x_{0})\}}=e^{\pm\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{0})}.

This proves the lemma for
k=0
. For other
k
, apply this to
(\psi_{x_{i+k}}^{p^{s}_{i+k},p^{u}_{i+k}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
,
(\psi_{y_{i+k}}^{q^{s}_{i+k},q^{u}_{i+k}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

∎

Remark:
The scheme of the proof only required that we have a uniform bound on the
S(\cdot)
parameter of a stable leaf infinitely often in the future of the chain. The scheme works similarly for chains which may not have a symbol recurring infinitely often, but lie within an irreducible component of
\Sigma
. The principle which allows for it is replacing the role of the recurring symbol with a concatenation arbitrarily far away with a fixed periodic word (which is possible by irreducibility).

Our next task is to show that if
\pi((\psi_{x_{i}}^{p^{s}_{i},p^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((\psi_{y_{i}}^{q^{s}_{i},q^{u}_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
then
C_{0}(x_{i})^{-1}:T_{x_{i}}M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d},C_{0}(y_{i})^{-1}:T_{y_{i}}M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
are “approximately the same.” There are two issues in expressing this formally:

(a)

C_{0}(x_{i})^{-1},C_{0}(y_{i})^{-1}
are linear maps with different domains.

(b)

C_{0}(\cdot)
is only determined up to orthogonal self maps of
H^{s}(\cdot),H^{u}(\cdot)
. In the two-dimensional case this means choosing a number from
\{\pm 1\}
, which commutes with composition of linear maps, but we wish to deal with the higher dimensional case, which is more subtle because of non-commutativity.

We address these issues in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.8
.

Under the assumptions of the previous lemma:

\exists\Xi_{i}:T_{x_{i}}M\rightarrow T_{y_{i}}M\text{ s.t }\forall\xi\in T_{x_{i}}M(1):\frac{|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{i})\xi|}{|C_{0}^{-1}(y_{i})\Xi_{i}\xi|}=e^{\pm 2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{i})},

where
\Xi_{i}
is an invertible linear transformation, and
\|\Xi_{i}\|,\|\Xi_{i}^{-1}\|\leq e^{Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(x_{i})}
.

Proof.

Using the notations from the previous lemma, define
\pi_{x,k}^{s}:=\pi_{x,k}:T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{s}_{k}\rightarrow H^{s}(x_{k}),\pi_{y,k}^{s}:=\pi_{y,k}:T_{f^{k}(p)}U^{s}_{k}\rightarrow H^{s}(y_{k})
. Let
V^{u}_{k}:=V^{u}((\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\leq k}),U^{u}_{k}:=V^{u}((\psi_{y_{i}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}})_{i\leq k})
, and define
\pi_{x,k}^{u}:=\pi_{x,k}:T_{f^{k}(p)}V^{u}_{k}\rightarrow H^{u}(x_{k}),\pi_{y,k}^{u}:=\pi_{y,k}:T_{f^{k}(p)}U^{u}_{k}\rightarrow H^{u}(y_{k})
as in the unstable case of Lemma
4.6
. Define
\Xi_{k}^{s}:H^{s}(x_{k})\rightarrow H^{s}(y_{k})
,
\Xi_{k}^{u}:H^{u}(x_{k})\rightarrow H^{u}(y_{k})
the following way:

\Xi_{k}^{s}:=\pi_{y,k}^{s}\circ(\pi_{x,k}^{s})^{-1}\text{ , }\Xi_{k}^{u}:=\pi_{y,k}^{u}\circ(\pi_{x,k}^{u})^{-1}.

The composition of
\pi_{y,k}^{s/u}\circ(\pi_{x,k}^{s/u})^{-1}
is well defined by the remark after Lemma
4.7
. Define
\Xi_{k}:T_{x_{k}}M\rightarrow T_{y_{k}}M
:

(46)

\forall\xi\in T_{x_{k}}M\text{: }\Xi_{k}\xi:=\Xi_{k}^{s}\xi_{s}+\Xi_{k}^{u}\xi_{u}\text{, where }\xi=\xi_{s}+\xi_{u},\xi_{s/u}\in H^{s/u}(x_{k}).

This is well defined since
T_{x_{k}}M=H^{s}(x_{k})\oplus H^{u}(x_{k})
. Notice that
\Xi_{k}[H^{s/u}(x_{k})]=H^{s/u}(y_{k})
. Hence, by Lemma
4.7
:

(47)

\frac{|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{k})\xi|}{|C_{0}^{-1}(y_{k})\Xi_{k}\xi|}=\sqrt{\frac{S^{2}(x_{k},\xi_{s})+U^{2}(x_{k},\xi_{u})}{S^{2}(y_{k},\Xi_{k}\xi_{s})+U^{2}(y_{k},\Xi_{k}\xi_{u})}}=e^{\pm 2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}.

It remains to bound
\|\Xi_{k}\|
and
\|\Xi_{k}^{-1}\|
. We begin by showing bounds on the norms of
\Xi_{k}^{s/u}
. By the bounds for
\|\pi_{x,k}^{s/u}\|
,
\|(\pi_{x,k}^{s/u})^{-1}\|
,
\|\pi_{y,k}^{s/u}\|
,
\|(\pi_{y,k}^{s/u})^{-1}\|

from Lemma
4.6

,

(48)

\|\Xi_{k}^{s/u}\|\leq\exp\Big(Q_{\epsilon}(x_{k})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y_{k})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\Big).

We continue to bound
\|\Xi_{k}\|
. To ease notations we will omit the ’
k
’ subscripts. Recall
\rho(M)
from §
2.2
. By Lemma
4.1
,
d(x,f^{k}(p))<\frac{\sqrt{d}}{100}Q_{\epsilon}(x)<\rho(M)
for
\epsilon
small enough. Similarly,
d(y,f^{k}(p))<\rho(M)
. Therefore the following is well defined:
\psi_{x}^{-1}(f^{k}(p))=:z_{x}^{\prime}
,
\psi_{y}^{-1}(f^{k}(p))=:z_{y}^{\prime}
. The vectors of the first
s(x)
coordinates of
z_{x}^{\prime}/z_{y}^{\prime}
will be called
z_{x}/z_{y}
respectively.

Part 1
: Let
\xi\in T_{x}M
,
\xi=\xi_{s}+\xi_{u}
,
\xi_{s/u}\in H^{s/u}(x)
, then
|\xi_{s}|,|\xi_{u}|\leq\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\xi|
.

Proof
: w.l.o.g
|\xi|=1
. Notice that the size of
\xi_{s/u}
can be very big, even when
|\xi_{s}+\xi_{u}|=|\xi|=1
. This can happen when

\sphericalangle(H^{s}(x),H^{u}(x)):=\inf\limits_{\eta_{s}\in H^{s}(x),\eta_{u}\in H^{u}(x)}|\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})|
is very small, and
\xi_{s}
and
\xi_{u}
are almost parallel, of the same size, and are pointing to almost opposite directions. Consider the triangle created by the tips of
\xi_{s}
and
-\xi_{u}
, and the origin. Denote its angle at the origin by
\alpha
(the angle between
\xi_{s}
and
-\xi_{u}
). The size of the edge in front of
\alpha
has length 1 (since
|\xi_{s}-(-\xi_{u})|=|\xi_{s}+\xi_{u}|=1
). Angles between two non-parallel vectors are in
(0,\pi)
, and their sine is always positive. By the sine theorem,

(49)

|\xi_{s}|,|\xi_{u}|\leq\frac{1}{|\sin\alpha|}\leq\frac{1}{\inf\limits_{\eta_{s}\in H^{s}(x),\eta_{u}\in H^{u}(x)}|\sin\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})|}=\frac{1}{\sin\sphericalangle(H^{s}(x),H^{u}(x))}.

Notice that
\frac{1}{|\sin\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})|}\leq\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|
,
\forall\eta_{s/u}\in H^{s/u}(x)
. To see this, notice first the identity

\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|^{2}=\sup_{\eta_{s}\in H^{s}(x),\eta_{u}\in H^{u}(x),|\eta_{s}+\eta_{u}|=1}\{S^{2}(x,\eta_{s})+U^{2}(x,\eta_{u})\}.

Choose
\eta_{s}\in H^{s}(x)(1),\eta_{u}\in H^{u}(x)(1)
which minimize
\sin\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})
. w.l.o.g
\cos\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})>0
. Let
\zeta:=\frac{\eta_{s}-\eta_{u}}{|\eta_{s}-\eta_{u}|}
. Since
C_{0}^{-1}(x)\eta_{s}\perp C_{0}^{-1}(x)\eta_{u}
and
\|C_{0}(x)\|\leq 1
,

|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\zeta|^{2}\geq\frac{|\eta_{s}|^{2}}{|\eta_{s}-\eta_{u}|^{2}}+\frac{|\eta_{u}|^{2}}{|\eta_{s}-\eta_{u}|^{2}}=\frac{2}{2-2\cos\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u}))}\geq\frac{1}{1-\cos^{2}\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})}=\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})}.

So
\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\geq\frac{1}{|\sin\sphericalangle(\eta_{s},\eta_{u})|}
. By (
49
),
|\xi_{s}|,|\xi_{u}|\leq\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|
.

QED

Part 2
:
\Xi=d_{C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})+E_{1}
, where
E_{1}:T_{x}M\rightarrow T_{y}M
is linear and
\|E_{1}\|\leq\frac{1}{2}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}
.

Proof
: We will begin by showing that the expression above is well defined for
\epsilon<\frac{\rho(M)}{2}
. This is true because
\exp_{x}[B_{\epsilon}(0)]\subset B_{\epsilon+d(x,y)}(y)\subset B_{2\epsilon}(y)
;
|C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}|=d(x,f^{k}(p))<\epsilon
; so
\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x}
is defined on an open neighborhood of
C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}
, and hence is differentiable on it.

By definition,
\Xi^{s}=\pi_{y,k}^{s}\circ(\pi_{x,k}^{s})^{-1}
.

•

\xi_{s}\in H^{s}(x)
is mapped by
(\pi_{x,k}^{s})^{-1}
to
\xi_{s}^{\prime}
, a tangent vector at
f^{k}(p)

given by
\xi_{s}^{\prime}=d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{z_{x}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)
, where
G
represents
V^{s}_{k}
in
\psi_{x_{k}}^{p_{k}^{s},p^{u}_{k}}
and
v=C_{0}^{-1}(x)\xi_{s}
. We write
\xi_{s}^{\prime}
as follows:

\xi_{s}^{\prime}=d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{z_{x}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)=(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{z_{x}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)=:d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}w\\
d_{z_{y}}Fw\\
\end{array}\Big),

for some
w
, where
F
is the representing function of
U^{s}_{k}
in
\psi_{y_{k}}^{q^{s}_{k},q^{u}_{k}}
. This representation is possible because
\xi_{s}^{\prime}
is tangent to the stable manifold
U^{s}_{k}
in
\psi_{y_{k}}^{q^{s}_{k},q^{u}_{k}}
at
f^{k}(p)=\psi_{y}(z_{y}^{\prime})
.

•

\xi_{s}^{\prime}=(\psi_{x,k}^{s})^{-1}\xi_{s}
is mapped by
\pi_{y,k}^{s}
to

\Xi^{s}\xi_{s}=\pi_{y,k}^{s}\xi_{s}^{\prime}=d_{0}\psi_{y}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}w\\
0\\
\end{array}\Big).

By construction,

\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}w\\
d_{z_{y}}Fw\\
\end{array}\Big)=(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}(d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x})\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}v\\
d_{z_{x}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big).

We abuse notation and use
w,v
to represent both vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{s(x)}\times\{0\}
and vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{s(x)}
. Then,

w=(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}v+\Big[(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
d_{z_{x}}Gv\\
\end{array}\Big)-\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
d_{z_{y}}Fw\\
\end{array}\Big)\Big].

Call the term in the brackets
E_{s}\xi_{s}
, then

\Xi_{s}=C_{\chi}(y)(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}C_{0}^{-1}(x)+C_{0}(y)E_{s}
.

•

\Xi^{s}\xi_{s}=d_{0}\psi_{y}\Big(\begin{array}[]{c}w\\
0\\
\end{array}\Big)\Rightarrow|w|=|(d_{0}\psi_{y})^{-1}\Xi^{s}\xi_{s}|=|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\Xi^{s}\xi_{s}|\leq\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\cdot\|\Xi^{s}\|\cdot|\xi_{s}|\leq

\leq\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\cdot e^{Q_{\epsilon}(x_{k})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y_{k})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}}|\xi_{s}|\leq 2\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\cdot|\xi_{s}|(\because\text{ \eqref{shluk} })
.

•

\mathrm{Lip}(G)\leq Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}},\mathrm{Lip}(F)\leq Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}
(by the remark after Definition
3.2
).

•

\xi_{s}=C_{\chi}(x)v\Rightarrow|v|\leq\|C_{\chi}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\xi_{s}|
.

Hence:

\displaystyle|C_{0}(y)E_{s}\xi_{s}|\leq

\displaystyle\|d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\exp_{y}^{-1}\|\cdot\|d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}\|\cdot\|d_{z_{x}}G\|\cdot|v|+\|d_{z_{y}}F\|\cdot|w|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cdot 2Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\cdot\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\xi_{s}|+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\cdot 2\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\cdot|\xi_{s}|

(50)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})|\xi_{s}|.

Similarly,
\Xi_{u}=C_{0}(y)(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}C_{0}^{-1}(x)+C_{0}(y)E_{u}
, where
E_{u}:H^{u}(x)\rightarrow T_{y}M
and

(51)

\|C_{0}(y)E_{u}\|\leq(Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}).

For a general tangent
\xi\in T_{x}M
, write
\xi=\xi_{s}+\xi_{u},\xi_{s/u}\in H^{s/u}(x)
, then

\displaystyle\Xi\xi=

\displaystyle\Xi(\xi_{s}+\xi_{u})=\Xi_{s}\xi_{s}+\Xi_{u}\xi_{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(C_{0}(y)(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}C_{0}^{-1}(x)+C_{0}(y)E_{s}\Big)\xi_{s}+\Big(C_{0}(y)(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}C_{0}^{-1}(x)+C_{0}(y)E_{u}\Big)\xi_{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{0}(y)(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}C_{0}^{-1}(x)(\xi_{s}+\xi_{u})+C_{0}(y)(E_{s}\xi_{s}+E_{u}\xi_{u})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{0}(y)(d_{z_{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{y})^{-1}d_{z_{x}^{\prime}}\psi_{x}C_{0}^{-1}(x)\xi+C_{0}(y)(E_{s}\xi_{s}+E_{u}\xi_{u}).

Hence, since
d_{z^{\prime}_{x}}\psi_{x}=d_{C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}}\exp_{x}\circ C_{0}(x),d_{z^{\prime}_{y}}\psi_{y}=d_{C_{0}(y)z_{y}^{\prime}}\exp_{y}\circ C_{0}(y)
, we get

\Xi\xi=d_{f^{k}(p)}\exp_{y}^{-1}d_{C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}}\exp_{x}\xi+C_{0}(y)(E_{s}\xi_{s}+E_{u}\xi_{u})\equiv d_{C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})\xi+E_{1}\xi,

where

\displaystyle|E_{1}\xi|\leq

\displaystyle|C_{0}(y)E_{s}\xi_{s}|+|C_{0}(y)E_{u}\xi_{u}|\leq((Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})|)(|\xi_{s}|+|\xi_{u}|)\text{ }(\because\text{ \eqref{Es},\eqref{Eu}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle((Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})|)(\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\xi|+\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot|\xi|)\text{ }(\because\text{Part 1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|(Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})|\xi|.

Therefore,

\Xi=d_{C_{0}(x)z_{x}^{\prime}}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})+E_{1}\Rightarrow\|\Xi\|\leq\|d_{\cdot}\exp_{y}^{-1}\|\cdot\|d_{\cdot}\exp_{x}\|+\|E_{1}\|\leq 4+2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|(Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}).

Now using this again in (
47
):

\displaystyle\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\leq

\displaystyle e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\Xi\|\leq e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\cdot\|\Xi\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\Big(4+2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|(Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\cdot e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}\Big(4+2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\cdot 2e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}(x_{k})}\Big(\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5(1-\epsilon)\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|+\epsilon\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\Rightarrow(1-\epsilon)\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\leq(1-\epsilon)5\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\Rightarrow\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|\leq 5\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|.

By symmetry we will also get
\|C_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\leq 5\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|
. In particular,
\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(y)}{Q_{\epsilon}(x)}=5^{2\gamma}
. Substituting these in the bounds for
\|E_{1}\|
, for all
\epsilon
small enough:

\displaystyle\|E_{1}\|\leq

\displaystyle 2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|Q_{\epsilon}(y)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}+2\cdot(5^{2\gamma})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\|C_{0}^{-1}(x)\|Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\leq\frac{1}{2}Q_{\epsilon}(x)^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}.

QED

Part 3
:
\forall u\in R_{\epsilon}(0)
:
\exists D\in\mathcal{D}
s.t
\|\Theta_{D}d_{C_{0}(x)u}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})-\Theta_{D}\|\leq Q_{\epsilon}(x)
(see Definition
2.16
).

Proof
: Begin by choosing
\epsilon
smaller than
\frac{\varpi(\mathcal{D})}{2\sqrt{d}}
(as in Definition
2.16
). Then
\exists D\in\mathcal{D}
such that
\exp_{x}[R_{\epsilon}(0)]\subset B_{\frac{\varpi(\mathcal{D})}{2}}(x)
, because
d(x,\exp_{x}C_{0}(x)v)=|C_{0}(x)v|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d}|v|_{\infty}<\epsilon\sqrt{d}
, so
\exp_{x}[R_{\epsilon}(0)]
has diameter less than
2\sqrt{d}\epsilon<\varpi(\mathcal{D})
. Hence the choice of
D
and of
\Theta_{D}
is proper. Since

\Theta_{D}\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x}=(\Theta_{D}\exp_{y}^{-1}-\Theta_{D}\exp_{x}^{-1})\exp_{x}+\Theta_{D},

we get that for any
u\in R_{\epsilon}(0)
:

\Theta_{D}d_{C_{0}(x)u}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})=d_{C_{0}(x)u}(\Theta_{D}\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})=d_{\exp_{x}C_{0}(x)u}(\Theta_{D}\exp_{y}^{-1}-\Theta_{D}\exp_{x}^{-1})d_{C_{0}(x)u}\exp_{x}+\Theta_{D}.

Then

\|\Theta_{D}d_{C_{0}(x)u}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})-\Theta_{D}\|\leq\|\Theta_{D}\exp_{y}^{-1}-\Theta_{D}\exp_{x}^{-1}\|_{C^{2}}\cdot\|d_{C_{0}(x)u}\exp_{x}\|\leq L_{2}d(x,y)\|d_{C_{0}(x)u}\exp_{x}\|,

where
L_{2}
is the uniform Lipschitz const. of
x\mapsto\nu_{x}^{-1}\exp_{x}^{-1}
(Proposition
2.17
). This is due to the fact that
d(x,\exp_{x}C_{0}(x)u)<\sqrt{d}\epsilon\Rightarrow\exp_{x}C_{0}(x)u\in D
. In addition,
\|d_{C_{0}(x)u}\exp_{x}\|\leq\|Id_{T_{x}M}\|+E_{0}|C_{0}(x)u-0|\leq 1+E_{0}\epsilon
(
E_{0}
is a constant introduced in Lemma
4.6
). Therefore, in total,

(52)

\|\Theta_{D}d_{C_{0}(x)u}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})-\Theta_{D}\|\leq L_{2}(1+\epsilon E_{0})\cdot d(x,y)<Q_{\epsilon}(x)\text{ }\because\text{Lemma \ref{firstchapter2}}.

QED

Adding the estimates of part 2 and part 3, we get

\displaystyle\|\Xi\|=

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{D}d_{C_{0}(x)v}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})-\Theta_{D}+\Theta_{D}+\Theta_{D}E_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{D}d_{C_{0}(x)v}(\exp_{y}^{-1}\exp_{x})-\Theta_{D}\|+\|\Theta_{D}\|+\|E_{1}\|\leq Q_{\epsilon}(x)+1+\frac{1}{2}Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(x)\leq e^{Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(x)}.

If we exchange the roles of
x_{k},y_{k}
then we get
\Xi_{k}^{-1}
, so
\|\Xi_{k}^{-1}\|
is also less than
e^{Q_{\epsilon}^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(x_{k})}
.
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

Under the assumptions and notations of the previous lemma:

\forall i\in\mathbb{Z}:\frac{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{i})\|}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(y_{i})\|}=e^{\pm(2\Gamma+1)Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}},

and so

\frac{Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})}{Q_{\epsilon}(y_{i})}=e^{\pm 2\gamma(2\Gamma+1)Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.8
:

\displaystyle\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{i})\|\leq

\displaystyle e^{2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}}\|C_{0}^{-1}(y_{i})\Xi_{i}\|\leq e^{(2\Gamma+1)Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}}\|C_{0}^{-1}(y_{i})\|,

and by symmetry we get the other inequality, and we are done.
∎

4.1.2.
Comparing frame parameters

Remark:
Notice that if
\gamma>\frac{5}{\beta}
, and
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small, then Corollary
4.9
in fact tell us

(53)

Q_{\epsilon}(y_{i})=I^{\pm 1}(Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})),\text{ }i\in\mathbb{Z}.

It is important that the bound we get on the resemblance of
Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})
and
Q_{\epsilon}(y_{i})
is of a form which commutes with the map
I
. This is the reason that it is important to require
\gamma>\frac{5}{\beta}
to make sure the bounds of Corollary
4.9
can be expressed in terms of
I
(
\geq\frac{5}{\beta}
is sufficient, if one carries the constants more carefully). To see where this commutativity relation is needed, see (
4.1.2
) below.

Corollary 4.10
.

\pi[\Sigma^{\#}]=\mathrm{RST}
.

Proof.

In Theorem
3.15
we saw
\pi[\Sigma^{\#}]\supseteq\mathrm{RST}
. By Corollary
4.5
, for every
\underline{u}=(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p^{s}_{i},p^{u}_{i}})_{\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
,
p:=\pi(\underline{u})\in 0
-summ.
\{p^{s}_{i}\wedge p^{u}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
is
I
-strongly subordinated to
\{Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})\}
by Lemma
2.24
. By the proof of Proposition
4.8
, the map
\pi_{x,i}:T_{f^{i}(p)}M\to T_{x_{k}}M
satisfies
\|\pi_{x,i}\|,\|\pi_{x,i}^{-1}\|\leq e^{Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})^{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}
. By the proof of Lemma
4.7
,
\frac{\|C_{0}^{-1}(f^{i}(p))\|}{\|C_{0}^{-1}(x_{i})\|}=e^{\pm 2\Gamma Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})^{\min\{\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{\gamma},\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\}}}
. As in (
53
), we get
Q_{\epsilon}(f^{i}(p))=I^{\pm 1}(Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i}))
. Then it follows that

I^{-1}(p_{i}^{s}\wedge p_{i}^{u})\leq I^{-1}(Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i}))\leq Q_{\epsilon}(f^{i}(x)).

Thus
p\in ST
(with the strongly tempered kernel
I^{-1}(p_{i}^{s}\wedge p_{i}^{u})
). Since
\limsup_{n\to\pm\infty}p_{i}^{s}\wedge p_{i}^{u}>0
,
p\in\mathrm{RST}
.
∎

Definition 4.11
.

(Compare with
[
Sar13
, Definition 8.1]
)

(1)

If
v
is a double chart, then
p^{u/s}(v)
means the
p^{u/s}
in
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}=v
.

(2)

A negative chain
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
is called
I
-maximal if it has a symbol recurring infinitely often, and

p^{u}(v_{0})\geq I^{-1}(p^{u}(u_{0}))

for every regular chain
(u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
for which there is a positive regular chain
(v_{i})_{i\geq 0}
s.t
\pi((u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
.

(3)

A positive chain
(v_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is called
I
-maximal if it has a symbol recurring infinitely often, and

p^{s}(v_{0})\geq I^{-1}(p^{s}(u_{0}))

for every chain
(u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
s.t
\pi((u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
.

Proposition 4.12
.

For every chain
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
,
(u_{i})_{i\leq 0}
and
(u_{i})_{i\geq 0}
are
I
-maximal.

Proof.

Step 1: Let
\underline{u},\underline{v}\in\Sigma^{\#}
be two chains s.t
\pi(\underline{u})=\pi(\underline{v})
. If
u_{0}=\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
and
v_{0}=\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
, then
Q_{\epsilon}(y)=I^{\pm 1}(Q_{\epsilon}(x))
, by (
53
).

Step 2: Every negative chain
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
with a symbol recurring infinitely often s.t
v_{0}=\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
where
p^{u}=Q_{\epsilon}(x)
is
I
-maximal, and every positive chain with a symbol recurring infinitely often
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
s.t
v_{0}=\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
where
p^{s}=Q_{\epsilon}(x)
is
I
-maximal.

Proof: Let
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
be a negative chain with a symbol recurring infinitely often, and assume
v_{0}=\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
where
p^{u}=Q_{\epsilon}(x)
. We show that it is
I
-maximal. Suppose
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
is an extension of
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
, and let
(u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
be a chain s.t
\pi((u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
. Write
u_{0}=\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
. Now, by step 1,
p^{u}=Q_{\epsilon}(x)\geq I^{-1}(Q_{\epsilon}(y))\geq I^{-1}(q^{u})
. The second half of step 2 is shown similarly.

Step 3: Let
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
be a negative chain with a symbol recurring infinitely often, and suppose
v_{0}\rightarrow v_{1}
. If
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
is
I
-maximal, then also
(v_{i})_{i\leq 1}
is
I
-maximal. Let
(v_{i})_{i\geq 0}
be a positive chain with a symbol recurring infinitely often, and suppose
v_{-1}\rightarrow v_{0}
. If
(v_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is
I
-maximal, then also
(v_{i})_{i\geq-1}
is
I
-maximal.

Proof: Let
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
be an
I
-maximal negative chain with a symbol recurring infinitely often, and suppose
v_{0}\rightarrow v_{1}
. Suppose that
(u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
and that
(v_{i})_{i\leq 1}
has a symbol repeating infinitely often, and that there is an extension
(v_{i})_{i\leq 1}
to a chain
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
s.t
\pi((u_{i+1})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((v_{i+1})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
. We write
v_{i}=\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}}
, and
u_{i}=\psi_{y_{1}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}}
; and we show that
p_{1}^{u}\geq I^{-1}(q_{1}^{u})
. Since
\pi((u_{i+1})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((v_{i+1})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
and
\pi\circ\sigma=f\circ\pi
:

\pi((u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}).

Therefore, since
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
is
I
-maximal,
p_{0}^{u}\geq I^{-1}(q_{0}^{u})
. Also, by step 1:
Q_{\epsilon}(x_{1})\geq I^{-1}(Q_{\epsilon}(y_{1}))
. It follows that

\displaystyle p_{1}^{u}=

\displaystyle\min\{I(p_{0}^{u}),Q_{\epsilon}(x_{1})\}\text{ }(\because v_{0}\rightarrow v_{1})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\{I(I^{-1}(q_{0}^{u})),I^{-1}(Q_{\epsilon}(y_{1}))\}

(54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I^{-1}(\min\{I(q_{0}^{u}),Q_{\epsilon}(y_{1})\})=I^{-1}(q_{1}^{u})\text{ }(\because u_{0}\rightarrow u_{1}\text{ and }I^{-1}\circ I=I\circ I^{-1}).

This proves the case of step 3 dealing with negative chains. The proof for the case of positive chains is shown similarly.

Step 4: Proof of the proposition: Suppose
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
, and write
v_{i}=\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}}
. Since
(v_{i})_{i_{\mathbb{Z}}}
is a chain, we get that
\{(p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u})\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}

is
I
-strongly subordinated to
\{Q_{\epsilon}(x_{i})\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Since
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
,
\limsup_{i\rightarrow\infty}(p_{i}^{s}\wedge p_{i}^{u})>0
. Therefore by Lemma
2.27
,
p_{n}^{u}=Q_{\epsilon}(x_{n})
for some
n<0
, and
p_{l}^{s}=Q_{\epsilon}(x_{l})
for some
l>0
. By step 2,
(v_{i})_{i\leq n}
is an
I
-maximal negative chain, and
(v_{i})_{i\geq l}
is an
I
-maximal positive chain. By step 3:
(v_{i})_{i\leq 0}
is an
I
-maximal negative chain, and
(v_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is an
I
-maximal positive chain.
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}},(\psi_{y_{i}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
be two chains s.t
\pi((\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\pi((\psi_{y_{i}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=p
. Then for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
p_{k}^{u}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{k}^{u}),p_{k}^{s}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{k}^{s})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.12
,
(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\leq 0}
is
I
-maximal, so
p_{0}^{u}\geq I^{-1}(q_{0}^{u})
.
(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\leq 0}
is also
I
-maximal, so
q_{0}^{u}\geq I^{-1}(p_{0}^{u})
. It follows that
p_{0}^{u}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{0}^{u})
. Similarly
p_{0}^{s}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{0}^{s})
. Working with the shifted sequences
(\psi_{x_{i+k}}^{p_{i+k}^{s},p_{i+k}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
, and
(\psi_{y_{i+k}}^{q_{i+k}^{s},q_{i+k}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
, we obtain
p_{k}^{u}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{k}^{u}),p_{k}^{s}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{k}^{s})
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

4.1.3.
Comparing Pesin charts

Theorem 4.14
.

The following holds for all
\epsilon
small enough. Suppose
(\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}},(\psi_{y_{i}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\Sigma^{\#}
are chains s.t
\pi((\psi_{x_{i}}^{p_{i}^{s},p_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=p=\pi((\psi_{y_{i}}^{q_{i}^{s},q_{i}^{u}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
then for all i:

(1)

d(x_{i},y_{i})<\epsilon
,

(2)

p_{i}^{u}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{i}^{u}),p_{i}^{s}=I^{\pm 1}(q_{i}^{s})
,

(3)

(\psi_{y_{i}}^{-1}\circ\psi_{x_{i}})(u)=O_{i}u+a_{i}+\Delta_{i}(u)
for all
u\in R_{\epsilon}(0)
, where
O_{i}\in O(d)
is a
d\times d
orthogonal matrix,
a_{i}
is a constant vector s.t
|a_{i}|_{\infty}\leq 10^{-1}(q_{i}^{u}\wedge q_{i}^{s})
, and
\Delta_{i}
is a vector field s.t
\Delta_{i}(0)=0,\|d_{v}\Delta_{i}\|<\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}
.
O_{i}
preserves
\mathbb{R}^{s(x)}
and
\mathbb{R}^{d-s(x)}
.
8
8

8

Recall, in our notations
\mathbb{R}^{s(x)}:=C_{0}^{-1}(x_{i})[H^{s}(x_{i})]=C_{0}^{-1}(y_{i})[H^{s}(y_{i})]\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbb{R}^{d-s(x)}:=(\mathbb{R}^{s(x)})^{\perp}=C_{0}^{-1}(x_{i})[H^{u}(x_{i})]=C_{0}^{-1}(y_{i})[H^{u}(y_{i})]
.

Proof.

Parts
(1)
and
(2)
are the content of Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.13
respectively; Part
(3)
is shown similarly to
[
BO18
, Theorem 4.13]
.
∎

4.2.
Similar charts have similar manifolds

4.2.1.
Comparing manifolds

Proposition 4.15
.

The following holds for all
\epsilon
small enough. Let
V^{s}
(resp.
U^{s}
) be an
s
-admissible manifold in
\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}}
(resp.
\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
). Suppose
V^{s},U^{s}
stay in windows. If
x=y
then either
V^{s},U^{s}
are disjoint, or one contains the other. The same statement holds for
u
-admissible manifolds.

Proof is similar to
[
BO18
, Proposition 4.15]
.

5.
Markov partitions and symbolic dynamics

In sections 1,2,3 of this paper we developed the shadowing theory needed to construct Markov partitions à la Bowen (
[
Bow78
,
Bow08
]
), following the improvements of Sarig to the shadowing theory
[
Sar13
]
, and its extension to higher dimensions
[
BO18
]
, while carrying it in a setup where hyperbolicity is replaced by
0
-summability. What remains is to carry out this construction. This is the content of this part. As in
[
BO18
]
, we follow
[
Bow78
]
and
[
Sar13
]
closely here.

5.1.
A locally finite countable Markov partition

5.1.1.
The cover

In §
2.4
we constructed a countable Markov shift
\Sigma
with countable alphabet
\mathcal{V}
and a uniformly continuous map
\pi:\Sigma\rightarrow M
which commutes with the left shift
\sigma:\Sigma\rightarrow\Sigma
, so that
\pi[\Sigma]\supseteq\mathrm{RST}
. Moreover, when

\Sigma^{\#}=\{u\in\Sigma:u\text{ is a regular chain}\}=\{u\in\Sigma:\exists v,w\in\mathcal{V}\exists n_{k},m_{k}\uparrow\infty\text{ s.t }u_{n_{k}}=v,u_{-m_{k}}=w\}

then
\pi[\Sigma^{\#}]\supset\mathrm{RST}
. However,
\pi
is not finite-to-one. In this section we study the following countable cover of
\mathrm{RST}
:

Definition 5.1
.

\mathcal{Z}:=\{Z(v):v\in\mathcal{V}\}
, where
Z(v):=\{\pi(u):u\in\Sigma^{\#},u_{0}=v\}
.

Theorem 5.2
.

For every
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
,
|\{Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}:Z^{\prime}\cap Z\neq\varnothing\}|<\infty
.

Proof.

Fix some
Z=Z(\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}})
. If
Z^{\prime}=Z(\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}})
intersects
Z
then there must exist two chains
v,w\in\Sigma^{\#}
s.t
v_{0}=\psi_{x}^{p^{s},p^{u}},w_{0}=\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}
and
\pi(v)=\pi(w)
. Lemma
4.13
says that in this case
q^{u}\geq I^{-1}(p^{u}),q^{s}\geq I^{-1}(p^{s})
.
It follows that
Z^{\prime}
belongs to
\{Z(\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}):\psi_{y}^{q^{s},q^{u}}\in\mathcal{V},q^{u}\wedge q^{s}\geq I^{-1}(p^{u}\wedge p^{s})\}
. By definition, the cardinality of this set is less than or equal to:

|\{\psi_{y}^{\eta}\in\mathcal{A}:\eta\geq I^{-1}(p^{s}\wedge p^{u})\}|\times|\{(q^{s},q^{u})\in\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I}:q^{s}\wedge q^{u}\geq I^{-1}(p^{s}\wedge p^{u})\}|.

This is a finite number because of the discreteness of
\mathcal{A}
(Proposition
2.20
).
∎

5.1.2.
Product structure

Suppose
x\in Z(v)\in\mathcal{Z}
, then
\exists\underline{u}\in\Sigma^{\#}
s.t
u_{0}=v
and
\pi(\underline{u})=x
. Associated to
\underline{u}
are two admissible manifolds in
v
:
V^{u}((u_{i})_{i\leq 0})
and
V^{s}((u_{i})_{i\geq 0})
. These manifolds do not depend on the choice of
\underline{u}
: If
\underline{w}\in\Sigma^{\#}
is another chain s.t
w_{0}=v
and
\pi(\underline{w})=x
then

V^{u}((u_{i})_{i\leq 0})=V^{u}((w_{i})_{i\leq 0})\text{ and }V^{s}((u_{i})_{i\geq 0})=V^{s}((w_{i})_{i\geq 0}),

because of Proposition
4.15
and the equalities
p^{s/u}(w_{0})=p^{s/u}(u_{0})=p^{s/u}(v)
. We are therefore free to make the following definitions:

Definition 5.3
.

Suppose
Z=Z(v)\in\mathcal{Z}
. For any
x\in Z
:

(1)

V^{u}(x,Z):=V^{u}((u_{i})_{i\leq 0})
for some (any)
\underline{u}\in\Sigma^{\#}
s.t
u_{0}=v
and
\pi(\underline{u})=x
.

We also set
W^{u}(x,Z):=V^{u}(x,Z)\cap Z
.

(2)

V^{s}(x,Z):=V^{s}((u_{i})_{i\geq 0})
for some (any)
\underline{u}\in\Sigma^{\#}
s.t
u_{0}=v
and
\pi(\underline{u})=x
.

We also set
W^{s}(x,Z):=V^{s}(x,Z)\cap Z
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Suppose
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
, then for any
x,y\in Z
:
V^{u}(x,Z),V^{u}(y,Z)
are either equal or they are disjoint. Similarly for
V^{s}(x,Z),V^{s}(y,Z)
; and for
W^{s}(x,Z),W^{s}(y,Z)
and for
W^{u}(x,Z),W^{u}(y,Z)
.

Proof.

The statement holds for
V^{s/u}(x,Z)
because of Proposition
4.15
. The statement for
W^{s/u}
is an immediate corollary.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.

Suppose
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
, then for any
x,y\in Z
:
V^{u}(x,Z)
and
V^{s}(y,Z)
intersect at a unique point
z
, and
z\in Z
. Thus
W^{u}(x,Z)\cap W^{s}(y,Z)=\{z\}
.

Proof.

See
[
Sar13
, Proposition 10.5]
.
∎

Definition 5.6
.

The Smale bracket of two points
x,y\in Z\in\mathcal{Z}
is the unique point
[x,y]_{Z}\in W^{u}(x,Z)\cap W^{s}(y,Z)
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose
x,y\in Z(v_{0})
, and
f(x),f(y)\in Z(v_{1})
. If
v_{0}\rightarrow v_{1}
then
f([x,y]_{Z(v_{0})})=[f(x),f(y)]_{Z(v_{1})}
.

Proof.

This is proved as in
[
Sar13
, Lemma 10.7]
using Theorem
3.6
(2).
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Suppose
Z,Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
. If
Z\cap Z^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
then for any
x\in Z,y\in Z^{\prime}
:
V^{s}(x,Z)
and
V^{s}(y,Z^{\prime})
intersect at a unique point.

We do not claim that this point is in
Z
nor
Z^{\prime}
.

Proof is similar to
[
BO18
, Lemma 5.8]
.

5.1.3.
The symbolic Markov property

Proposition 5.9
.

If
x=\pi((v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})
, where
\underline{v}\in\Sigma^{\#}
, then
f[W^{s}(x,Z(v_{0}))]\subset W^{s}(f(x),Z(v_{1}))

and
f^{-1}[W^{u}(f(x),Z(v_{1}))]\subset W^{u}(x,Z(v_{0}))
.

Proof.

See
[
Sar13
, Proposition 10.9]
.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Suppose
Z,Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
and
Z\cap Z^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
, then:

(1)

If
Z=Z(\psi_{x_{0}}^{p_{0}^{s},p_{0}^{u}})
and
Z^{\prime}=Z(\psi_{y_{0}}^{q_{0}^{s},q_{0}^{u}})
then
Z\subset\psi_{y_{0}}[R_{q_{0}^{s}\wedge q_{0}^{u}}(0)]

(2)

For any
x\in Z\cap Z^{\prime}
:
W^{u}(x,Z)\subset V^{u}(x,Z^{\prime})
and
W^{s}(x,Z)\subset V^{s}(x,Z^{\prime})

Proof is similar to
